TOOLKIT & METHODOLOGY\n\n## 3.1 TOOL SELECTION PRINCIPLES\n\n- IMAGE TOOL PRIORITY:\n  * For any request involving image creation, modification, style change, or visual design, ALWAYS prefer the Image Tool.\n  * Do NOT describe images purely in text when an image output is expected.\n  * If an image result is required, the Image Tool is mandatory.\n\n- CLI TOOLS PREFERENCE:\n  * Always prefer CLI tools over Python scripts when possible\n  * CLI tools are generally faster and more efficient for:\n    1. Synchronous Commands (blocking):\n      * You can omit `blocking`, as it defaults to true\n      * Use for quick operations that complete within 60 seconds\n      * Commands run directly and wait for completion\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="default" blocking="true">\n        ls -l\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n        (or simply omit the blocking parameter as it defaults to true)\n      * IMPORTANT: Do not use for long-running operations as they will timeout after 60 seconds\n\n  2. Asynchronous Commands (non-blocking):\n      * Use `blocking="false"` for any command that might take longer than 60 seconds or for starting background services\n      * Commands run in background and return immediately\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="dev" blocking="false">\n        npm run dev\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n      * Common use cases:\n        - Development servers (Next.js, React, etc.)\n        - Build processes\n        - Long-running data processing\n        - Background services\n\n- Session Management:\n  * Each command must specify a session_name\n  * Use consistent session names for related commands\n  * Different sessions are isolated from each other\n  * Example: Use "build" session for build commands, "dev" for development servers\n  * Sessions maintain state between commands\n\n- Command Execution Guidelines:\n  * For commands that might take longer than 60 seconds, ALWAYS use run_async="true"\n  * Do not rely on increasing timeout for long-running commands\n  * Use proper session names for organization\n  * Chain commands with && for sequential execution\n  * Use | for piping output between commands\n  * Redirect output to files for long-running processes\n\n- Avoid commands requiring confirmation; actively use -y or -f flags for automatic confirmation\n- Avoid commands with excessive output; save to files when necessary\n- Chain multiple commands with operators to minimize interruptions and improve efficiency:\n  1. Use && for sequential execution: `command1 && command2 && command3`\n  2. Use || for fallback execution: `command1 || command2`\n  3. Use ; for unconditional execution: `command1; command2`\n  4. Use | for piping output: `command1 | command2`\n  5. Use > and >> for output redirection: `command > file` or `command >> file`\n- Use pipe operator to pass command outputs, simplifying operations\n- Use non-interactive `bc` for simple calculations, Python for complex math; never calculate mentally\n- Use `uptime` command when users explicitly request sandbox status check or wake-up\n\n## 3.3 CODE DEVELOPMENT PRACTICES\n- CODING:\n  * Must save code to files before execution; direct code input to interpreter commands is forbidden\n  * Write Python code for complex mathematical calculations and analysis\n  * Use search tools to find solutions when encountering unfamiliar problems\n  * For index.html, use deployment tools directly, or package everything into a zip file and provide it as a message attachment\n  * When creating web interfaces, always create CSS files first before HTML to ensure proper styling and design consistency\n  * For images, use real image URLs from sources like unsplash.com, pexels.com, pixabay.com, giphy.com, or wikimedia.org instead of creating placeholder images; use placeholder.com only as a last resort\n\n- WEBSITE DEPLOYMENT:\n  * Only use the \'deploy\' tool when users explicitly request permanent deployment to a production environment\n  * The deploy tool publishes static HTML+CSS+JS sites to a public URL using S3 web Pages\n  * If the same name is used for deployment, it will redeploy to the same project as before\n  * For temporary or development purposes, serve files locally instead of using the deployment tool\n  * When editing HTML files, always share the preview URL provided by the automatically running HTTP server with the user\n  * The preview URL is automatically generated and available in the tool results when creating or editing HTML files\n  * When deploying, ensure all assets (images, scripts, stylesheets) use relative paths to work correctly\n\n- PYTHON EXECUTION: Create reusable modules with proper error handling and logging. Use -nopgbrk to remove page breaks\n  2. pdfinfo: Get PDF metadata\n    - Use to check PDF properties\n    - Extract page count and dimensions\n  3. pdfimages: Extract images from PDFs\n    - Use -j to convert to JPEG\n    - Use for text replacement\n    - Use for pattern matching\n- File Analysis:\n  1. file: Determine file type\n  2. wc: Count words/lines\n  3. head/tail: View file parts\n  4. less: View large files\n- Data Processing:\n  1. jq: JSON processing\n    - Use -n to specify number of lines\n    - Use -f to follow file changes\n  3. awk: Pattern scanning and processing\n    - Use for column-based data processing\n    - Use for complex text transformations\n  4. find: Locate files and directories\n    - Use for precise text matching\n  2. Combine with CLI tools for powerful searches\n  3. Save complex patterns to files for reuse\n  4. Test patterns with small samples first\n  5. Each section contains specific, actionable subtasks based on complexity - use only as many as needed, no more\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Adapt the plan as needed while maintaining its integrity as your execution compass\n\n## 5.2 TODO.MD FILE STRUCTURE AND USAGE\nThe todo.md file is your primary working document and action plan:\n\n1. Contains the complete list of tasks you MUST complete to fulfill the user\'s request\n2. Format with clear sections, each containing specific tasks marked with [ ] (incomplete) or [x] (complete)\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Before every action, consult your todo.md to determine which task to tackle next\n6. The todo.md serves as your instruction set - if a task is in todo.md, you are responsible for completing it\n7. Update the todo.md as you make progress, adding new tasks as needed and marking completed ones\n8. Never delete tasks from todo.md - instead mark them complete with [x] to maintain a record of your work\n9. Once ALL tasks in todo.md are marked complete [x], you MUST call either the \'complete\' state or \'ask\' tool to signal task completion\n10. SCOPE CONSTRAINT: Focus on completing existing tasks before adding new ones; avoid continuously expanding scope\n11. CAPABILITY AWARENESS: Only add tasks that are achievable with your available tools and capabilities\n12. FINALITY: After marking a section complete, do not reopen it or add new tasks unless explicitly directed by the user\n13. STOPPING CONDITION: If you\'ve made 3 consecutive updates to todo.md without completing any tasks, reassess your approach and either simplify your plan or **use the \'ask\' tool to seek user guidance.**\n14. COMPLETION VERIFICATION: Only mark a task as [x] complete when you have concrete evidence of completion\n15. SIMPLICITY: Keep your todo.md lean and direct with clear actions, avoiding unnecessary verbosity or granularity\n\n## 5.3 EXECUTION PHILOSOPHY\nYour approach is deliberately methodical and persistent:\n\n1. Operate in a continuous loop until explicitly stopped\n2. Execute one step at a time, following a consistent loop: evaluate state → select tool → execute → provide narrative update → track progress\n3. Every action is guided by your todo.md, consulting it before selecting any tool\n4. Thoroughly verify each completed step before moving forward\n5. **Provide Markdown-formatted narrative updates directly in your responses** to keep the user informed of your progress, explain your thinking, and clarify the next steps. Use headers, brief descriptions, and context to make your process transparent.\n6. CRITICALLY IMPORTANT: Continue running in a loop until either:\n  - Using the **\'ask\' tool (THE ONLY TOOL THE USER CAN RESPOND TO)** to wait for essential user input (this pauses the loop)\n  - Using the \'complete\' tool when ALL tasks are finished\n7. For casual conversation:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** to properly end the conversation and wait for user input (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n8. For tasks:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** when you need essential user input to proceed (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n  - Provide **narrative updates** frequently in your responses to keep the user informed without requiring their input\n  - Use \'complete\' only when ALL tasks are finished\n9. MANDATORY COMPLETION:\n  - IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after ALL tasks in todo.md are marked [x]\n  - NO additional commands or verifications after all tasks are complete\n  - NO further exploration or information gathering after completion\n  - NO redundant checks or validations after completion\n  - FAILURE to use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after task completion is a critical error\n\n## 5.4 TASK MANAGEMENT CYCLE\n1. STATE EVALUATION: Examine Todo.md for priorities, analyze recent Tool Results for environment understanding, and review past actions for context\n2. TOOL SELECTION: Choose exactly one tool that advances the current todo item\n3. EXECUTION: Wait for tool execution and observe results\n4. **NARRATIVE UPDATE:** Provide a **Markdown-formatted** narrative update directly in your response before the next tool call. Include explanations of what you\'ve done, what you\'re about to do, and why. Use headers, brief paragraphs, and formatting to enhance readability.\n5. PROGRESS TRACKING: Update todo.md with completed items and new tasks\n6. METHODICAL ITERATION: Repeat until section completion\n7. SECTION TRANSITION: Document completion and move to next section\n8. COMPLETION: IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' when ALL tasks are finished\n\n# 6. CONTENT CREATION\n\n## 6.1 WRITING GUIDELINES\n- Write content in continuous paragraphs using varied sentence lengths for engaging prose; avoid list formatting\n- Use prose and paragraphs by default; BROWSER AUTOMATION TOOLS\n\n- BROWSER CONTROL: All browser interaction goes through `browser-tool <action> [args...]` via `execute-command`.\n  * The browser (Chromium) is already running. Each command connects, acts, observes, and disconnects.\n\n- WORKFLOW (observe-act loop):\n  1. Run an action (e.g. navigate, click, fill). The tool automatically observes the page after every action.\n  2. Read the output: URL, title, interactive elements list, accessibility tree, and screenshot path.\n  3. Reference elements by their `[index]` number from the elements list (e.g. `click "[3]"`).\n  4. Use `see-image` on the screenshot path when you need to verify visual layout or images.\n\n- AVAILABLE COMMANDS:\n  * `observe` - View current page state without acting\n  * `navigate <url>` - Go to a URL\n  * `click <selector>` - Click an element (use `[N]` for element index, or CSS selector)\n  * `fill <selector> <text>` - Clear a field and fill it with text\n  * `type <selector> <text>` - Type text character by character\n  * `press <key>` - Press a key (Enter, Tab, Escape, etc.)\n  * `select <selector> <value>` - Select a dropdown option\n  * `scroll_down [px]` / `scroll_up [px]` - Scroll (default 500px)\n  * `scroll_to_text <text>` - Scroll until text is visible\n  * `go_back` - Navigate back\n  * `wait [seconds]` - Wait (default 2s)\n  * `extract_text [selector]` - Extract text content from page or element\n  * `extract_links [selector]` - Extract all links\n  * `screenshot [filename]` - Take a manual screenshot\n  * `dismiss_overlay` - Dismiss cookie banners or modals\n  * `js <script>` - Execute JavaScript on the page\n\n- CAPTCHA / HUMAN ASSISTANCE: If you encounter a CAPTCHA, verification, or any situation where automated interaction is stuck, MUST use `web-browser-takeover` to hand control to the user.\n\n# 12. ROUND TIMER\r\n===============\r\n\r\nEvery round has its own countdown timer.\r\n\r\nDisplay:\r\n\r\n* Current round\r\n* Remaining time\r\n\r\nThe timer should be clearly visible.\r\n\r\nWhen time is low:\r\n\r\n* Add visual warning\r\n* Change timer appearance\r\n* Add subtle warning animation\r\n* Optional warning sound\r\n\r\nWhen timer reaches 0:\r\n\r\n* Stop the player from launching new claw attempts\r\n* Allow the current claw action to resolve cleanly if appropriate\r\n* Calculate final score\r\n* Calculate stars\r\n* Show the results screen\r\n\r\nThe timer must be tested carefully so the game does not get stuck if it reaches 0 while the claw is:\r\n\r\n* Extended\r\n* Retrieving an object\r\n* Returning to the player\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n12. TEN-ROUND DIFFICULTY PROGRESSION\r\n====================================\r\n\r\nThere are exactly 10 rounds.\r\n\r\nEach round must be harder than the previous round, but difficulty should increase through:\r\n\r\n* Object placement\r\n* Object density\r\n* More obstacles\r\n* More moving objects\r\n* More worms\r\n* More difficult timing\r\n* Higher score requirements\r\n\r\nDo NOT make the game harder simply by making objects randomly impossible to reach.\r\n\r\nSuggested progression:\r\n\r\nROUND 1:\r\n\r\n* Basic Gold\r\n* Rocks\r\n* Simple layout\r\n\r\nROUND 2:\r\n\r\n* More Rocks\r\n* More Large Gold\r\n\r\nROUND 3:\r\n\r\n* More valuable targets\r\n* Introduce Mystery Bags\r\n\r\nROUND 4:\r\n\r\n* Introduce Moving Diamonds\r\n\r\nROUND 5:\r\n\r\n* Introduce Worms\r\n\r\nROUND 6:\r\n\r\n* More obstacles\r\n* More complex layouts\r\n\r\nROUND 7:\r\n\r\n* Faster moving objects\r\n* More Worms\r\n\r\nROUND 8:\r\n\r\n* More difficult object placement\r\n* More moving obstacles\r\n\r\nROUND 9:\r\n\r\n* High-value objects placed in difficult positions\r\n* Greater need for TNT and Lucky Leaf\r\n\r\nROUND 10:\r\n\r\n* Final challenge\r\n* Uses all major mechanics\r\n* Should feel like the climax of the game\r\n* Difficult but fair\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n13. LEVEL DESIGN AND CONTROLLED RANDOMIZATION\r\n=============================================\r\n\r\nTHIS IS VERY IMPORTANT.\r\n\r\nDo NOT generate every level completely randomly.\r\n\r\nDo NOT make every round use exactly one fixed layout.\r\n\r\nInstead, each round must have 3–5 predefined layouts designed by the developer.\r\n\r\nExample:\r\n\r\nROUND 5:\r\n\r\n* Layout A\r\n* Layout B\r\n* Layout C\r\n* Layout D\r\n\r\nWhen Round 5 starts:\r\n\r\n1. Randomly select one of the predefined layouts.\r\n2. Load that layout.\r\n3. Apply small controlled random variations if appropriate.\r\n\r\nEach predefined layout should be manually balanced.\r\n\r\nExample:\r\n\r\nA layout can define:\r\n\r\n* Object positions\r\n* Object types\r\n* Object movement paths\r\n* Worm positions\r\n* Rock positions\r\n* Treasure positions\r\n* Special object positions\r\n\r\nControlled random variations may include:\r\n\r\n* Small position offsets\r\n* Choosing between Small Gold and Medium Gold in designated slots\r\n* Slight movement speed variation\r\n* Choosing between predefined object variants\r\n\r\nDo NOT randomly generate an entire level from scratch.\r\n\r\nThe goal is:\r\nDESIGNED STRUCTURE + CONTROLLED RANDOMNESS\r\n\r\nEvery possible layout must be:\r\n\r\n* Fair\r\n* Beatably balanced\r\n* Capable of reaching the minimum target\r\n* Capable of reaching 3 stars with skilled play\r\n\r\nFor example, if Round 5 requires 7000 gold for 3 stars:\r\n\r\nLayout A:\r\nMaximum realistic skilled score ≈ 9000\r\n\r\nLayout B:\r\nMaximum realistic skilled score ≈ 8500\r\n\r\nLayout C:\r\nMaximum realistic skilled score ≈ 9000\r\n\r\nAll layouts must allow a skilled player to achieve 3 stars.\r\n\r\nDo NOT create a layout where 3 stars are mathematically impossible due to random object placement.\r\n\r\nThis system should also create replayability:\r\n\r\n* The player can replay the same round\r\n* A different predefined layout may be selected\r\n* The player can improve their star rating\r\n* The experience does not become completely predictable\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n14. THREE-STAR SCORING SYSTEM\r\n=============================\r\n\r\nImplement a Candy Crush-inspired three-star scoring system.\r\n\r\nEach round has:\r\n\r\n* Minimum passing target\r\n* 1-star threshold\r\n* 2-star threshold\r\n* 3-star threshold\r\n\r\nExample:\r\n\r\nMinimum target:\r\n1500 gold\r\n\r\n1 Star:\r\n2000 gold\r\n\r\n2 Stars:\r\n4500 gold\r\n\r\n3 Stars:\r\n7000 gold\r\n\r\nThresholds must increase appropriately for later rounds.\r\n\r\nDuring gameplay, display a vertical or stylized progress meter.\r\n\r\nThe meter should visually fill as the player earns gold.\r\n\r\nExample:\r\n\r\n```\r\n    ⭐ 3 Stars\r\n    │\r\n    │\r\n    █████████\r\n    │\r\n    ⭐ 2 Stars\r\n    │\r\n    █████\r\n    │\r\n    ⭐ 1 Star\r\n    │\r\n    █\r\n    │\r\n    0 Gold\r\n```\r\n\r\nAs the player\'s gold increases:\r\n\r\n* Animate the meter filling\r\n* Show the player\'s current gold\r\n* Show upcoming star thresholds\r\n\r\nAt the end:\r\n\r\n* Animate the final progress\r\n* Reveal stars one by one\r\n* Display 1, 2, or 3 stars\r\n\r\nImportant:\r\n\r\nMinimum target = pass the round\r\n\r\n1 Star = basic performance\r\n\r\n2 Stars = good performance\r\n\r\n3 Stars = excellent performance\r\n\r\nA player should be able to replay completed rounds to improve their star rating.\r\n\r\nUse localStorage to save:\r\n\r\n* Best score for each round\r\n* Best star rating for each round\r\n\r\nTotal possible stars:\r\n30\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n15. OPTIONAL COMBO SYSTEM\r\n=========================\r\n\r\nAdd a simple combo system.\r\n\r\nIf the player collects valuable objects consecutively without collecting:\r\n\r\n* Rock\r\n* Worm\r\n* Other low-value obstacles\r\n\r\nIncrease the combo.\r\n\r\nSuggested:\r\n\r\n3 consecutive valuable objects:\r\n+10% bonus\r\n\r\n5 consecutive valuable objects:\r\n+20% bonus\r\n\r\n8 consecutive valuable objects:\r\n+30% bonus\r\n\r\nReset combo when:\r\n\r\n* Player collects a Worm\r\n* Player collects a Rock\r\n* Player misses an object\r\n\r\nKeep the combo system simple.\r\n\r\nThe combo should reward skilled play without becoming the main focus of the game.\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n16. SECTION STRUCTURE\r\n=====================\r\n\r\nDivide the 10 rounds into themed sections.\r\n\r\nSECTION 1:\r\nRounds 1–3\r\n"The Old Mine"\r\n\r\nSECTION 2:\r\nRounds 4–6\r\n"The Lost Jungle"\r\n\r\nSECTION 3:\r\nRounds 7–9\r\n"Ancient Ruins"\r\n\r\nFINAL:\r\nRound 10\r\n"The Golden Temple"\r\n\r\nEach section should have:\r\n\r\n* Different background\r\n* Slightly different visual atmosphere\r\n* Appropriate object placement\r\n\r\nAt the end of each section:\r\n\r\n* Show a progression milestone\r\n* Display total stars earned\r\n* Display section completion\r\n* Continue to the next section\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n17. HUD\r\n=======\r\n\r\nDuring gameplay, display:\r\n\r\n* Round number\r\n* Current gold\r\n* Timer\r\n* Star progress meter\r\n* TNT quantity\r\n* Shield quantity\r\n* Lucky Leaf quantity\r\n* Claw Speed upgrade level\r\n* Combo counter when active\r\n\r\nThe HUD must not obscure important gameplay.\r\n\r\nUse clear icons and readable text.\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n18. RESULTS / WINNING SCREEN\r\n============================\r\n\r\nAfter each round:\r\n\r\nDisplay:\r\n\r\nROUND COMPLETE!\r\n\r\nGold Earned:\r\nXXXX\r\n\r\nStar Progress:\r\n[Animated progress meter]\r\n\r\nStars Earned:\r\n⭐ ⭐ ⭐\r\n\r\nAlso display:\r\n\r\n* Best previous score\r\n* Best previous star rating\r\n\r\nButtons:\r\n\r\n* Continue to Shop\r\n* Replay Round\r\n\r\nIf the player fails the minimum target:\r\n\r\nDisplay:\r\nROUND FAILED\r\n\r\nShow:\r\n\r\n* Gold earned\r\n* Minimum target\r\n* Current best score\r\n\r\nButtons:\r\n\r\n* Replay Round\r\n* Return to Shop if appropriate\r\n\r\nDo not erase gold earned in previous completed rounds.\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n19. FINAL SCREEN\r\n================\r\n\r\nAfter Round 10:\r\n\r\nDisplay:\r\n\r\nCONGRATULATIONS!\r\n\r\nYou completed all 10 rounds!\r\n\r\nTotal Gold Earned:\r\nXXXX\r\n\r\nTotal Stars:\r\nXX / 30\r\n\r\nThree-Star Rounds:\r\nX / 10\r\n\r\nShow:\r\n\r\n* Final performance\r\n* Total stars\r\n* Number of 3-star rounds\r\n* Replay button\r\n* Restart Game button\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n20. SAVE SYSTEM\r\n===============\r\n\r\nUse localStorage.\r\n\r\nSave:\r\n\r\n* Best score for each round\r\n* Best star rating for each round\r\n* Total stars\r\n* Completed rounds\r\n* Purchased permanent Claw Speed upgrade\r\n* Current consumable inventory if appropriate\r\n\r\nThe game must correctly restore saved progress after browser refresh.\r\n\r\nAdd a reset-save option in an appropriate menu.\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n21. AUDIO\r\n=========\r\n\r\nCreate an AudioManager system with placeholders for:\r\n\r\n* Background music\r\n* Claw launch\r\n* Gold collection\r\n* Diamond collection\r\n* Rock collection\r\n* Mystery Bag\r\n* TNT explosion\r\n* Shield activation\r\n* Lucky Leaf activation\r\n* Timer warning\r\n* Star achievement\r\n* Round completion\r\n* Shop ambience\r\n\r\nIf actual audio files are unavailable:\r\n\r\n* Create the system with placeholder paths\r\n* Make it easy to add audio files later\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n22. VISUAL FEEDBACK\r\n===================\r\n\r\nAdd visual feedback for:\r\n\r\n* Claw launch\r\n* Object collection\r\n* Gold earned\r\n* TNT explosion\r\n* Shield activation\r\n* Lucky Leaf transformation\r\n* Combo increase\r\n* Star threshold reached\r\n* Round completion\r\n* Shop purchase\r\n\r\nThe game should feel responsive and satisfying.\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n23. CONTROLS\r\n============\r\n\r\nKeyboard:\r\n\r\nSpace:\r\nLaunch claw\r\n\r\nT:\r\nUse TNT\r\n\r\nL:\r\nActivate Lucky Leaf\r\n\r\nEscape:\r\nPause game\r\n\r\nMouse:\r\n\r\nClick canvas:\r\nLaunch claw\r\n\r\nClick TNT button:\r\nUse TNT\r\n\r\nClick Lucky Leaf button:\r\nActivate Lucky Leaf\r\n\r\nMake sure controls do not conflict with browser behavior.\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n24. GAME ARCHITECTURE\r\n=====================\r\n\r\nUse modular JavaScript systems/classes where appropriate.\r\n\r\nSuggested systems:\r\n\r\nGame\r\nPlayer\r\nClaw\r\nGameObject\r\nGold\r\nRock\r\nDiamond\r\nMysteryBag\r\nWorm\r\nTNT\r\nGoldenTreasure\r\nLevelManager\r\nLayoutManager\r\nShop\r\nInventory\r\nUpgradeSystem\r\nStarSystem\r\nComboSystem\r\nSaveManager\r\nAudioManager\r\nUIManager\r\n\r\nThe LayoutManager should be responsible for:\r\n\r\n* Selecting one of the predefined layouts\r\n* Loading object positions\r\n* Applying controlled random variations\r\n* Ensuring the selected layout is valid\r\n\r\nDo not over-engineer the project.\r\n\r\nUse the simplest architecture that remains maintainable.\r\n\r\n==================================================\r\n25. // px per second (down)\\n    this.basePullSpeed = 260; // base pull speed px/s\\n    this.pullSpeed = this.basePullSpeed; // current pull speed (set when grabbing object)\\n\\n    this.heldObject = null;\\n    // claw head position cache\\n    this.headX = this.anchorX;\\n    this.headY = this.anchorY;\\n    this.grabRadius = 18;\\n  }\\n\\n  reset() {\\n    this.angle = 0;\\n    this.swingDir = 1;\\n    this.state = ClawState.SWINGING;\\n    this.ropeLength = 0;\\n    this.heldObject = null;\\n    this.pullSpeed = this.basePullSpeed;\\n  }\\n\\n  get headPosition() {\\n    return {\\n      x: this.anchorX + Math.sin(this.angle) * this.ropeLength,\\n      y: this.anchorY + Math.cos(this.angle) * this.ropeLength\\n    };\\n  }\\n\\n  // Retrival speed multiplier from upgrade level (1..5)\\n  setRetrievalLevel(level) {\\n    const mult = [1.0, 1.1, 1.2, 1.35, 1.5][Math.min(Math.max(level - 1, 0), 4)];\\n    this.basePullSpeed = 260 * mult;\\n  }\\n\\n  canLaunch() { return this.state === ClawState.SWINGING; }\\n\\n  launch() {\\n    if (this.state !== ClawState.SWINGING) return false;\\n    this.state = ClawState.EXTENDING;\\n    this.ropeLength = 0;\\n    AudioManager.sfx.launch();\\n    return true;\\n  }\\n\\n  update(dt, objects, callbacks) {\\n    if (this.state === ClawState.SWINGING) {\\n      this.angle += this.swingDir * this.swingSpeed * dt;\\n      if (this.angle >= this.maxAngle) { this.angle = this.maxAngle; this.swingDir = -1; }\\n      else if (this.angle <= this.minAngle) { this.angle = this.minAngle; this.swingDir = 1; }\\n      this.ropeLength = 0;\\n    } else if (this.state === ClawState.EXTENDING) {\\n      this.ropeLength += this.extendSpeed * dt;\\n      const head = this.headPosition;\\n      // collision check\\n      let hit = null;\\n      for (const obj of objects) {\\n        if (obj.removed || obj.collected) continue;\\n        if (obj.contains(head.x, head.y)) { hit = obj; break; }\\n      }\\n      if (hit) {\\n        this.heldObject = hit;\\n        hit.collected = true;\\n        this.state = ClawState.RETRACTING;\\n        // pull speed depends on the object\'s weight (smaller value = faster)\\n        this.pullSpeed = this.basePullSpeed * hit.pullSpeed;\\n        AudioManager.sfx.collect();\\n        if (callbacks.onGrab) callbacks.onGrab(hit);\\n      } else if (this.ropeLength >= this.maxRope) {\\n        this.state = ClawState.RETRACTING;\\n        this.pullSpeed = this.basePullSpeed * 2.0; // empty claw pulls fast\\n      }\\n    } else if (this.state === ClawState.RETRACTING) {\\n      this.ropeLength -= this.pullSpeed * dt;\\n      if (this.heldObject) {\\n        const head = this.headPosition;\\n        this.heldObject.x = head.x;\\n        this.heldObject.y = head.y;\\n      }\\n      if (this.ropeLength <= 0) {\\n        this.ropeLength = 0;\\n        const obj = this.heldObject;\\n        this.heldObject = null;\\n        this.state = ClawState.SWINGING;\\n        if (callbacks.onRetrieve) callbacks.onRetrieve(obj);\\n      }\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  draw(ctx) {\\n    const head = this.headPosition;\\n    // rope\\n    ctx.strokeStyle = \\"#d4b890\\";\\n    ctx.lineWidth = 2.5;\\n    ctx.beginPath();\\n    ctx.moveTo(this.anchorX, this.anchorY);\\n    ctx.lineTo(head.x, head.y);\\n    ctx.stroke();\\n\\n    // claw head\\n    ctx.save();\\n    ctx.translate(head.x, head.y);\\n    const rot = this.angle;\\n    ctx.rotate(rot);\\n    // claw housing\\n    ctx.fillStyle = \\"#888\\";\\n    ctx.fillRect(-10, -4, 20, 10);\\n    ctx.strokeStyle = \\"#555\\";\\n    ctx.lineWidth = 1.5;\\n    ctx.strokeRect(-10, -4, 20, 10);\\n    // pincers\\n    ctx.strokeStyle = \\"#ccc\\";\\n    ctx.lineWidth = 4;\\n    ctx.lineCap = \\"round\\";\\n    const open = this.state === ClawState.SWINGING ? 14 : (this.heldObject ? 4 : 10);\\n    ctx.beginPath();\\n    ctx.moveTo(-8, 6); && this.claw.state === ClawState.SWINGING) {\\n      this.endRound();\\n      return;\\n    }\\n\\n    // Particles\\n    this.particles = this.particles.filter(p => p.life > 0);\\n    for (const p of this.particles) p.update(dt);\\n\\n    // Floating toasts\\n    this.toasts = this.toasts.filter(t => t.life > 0);\\n    for (const t of this.toasts) { t.y -= 20 * dt; t.life -= dt; }\\n\\n    // Flash decay\\n    if (this.flashAlpha > 0) this.flashAlpha = Math.max(0, this.flashAlpha - dt * 2);\\n    if (this.shakeTime > 0) this.shakeTime = Math.max(0, this.shakeTime - dt);\\n\\n    this.updateHUD();\\n    this.updateStarMeter();\\n  }\\n\\n  // ---------- OBJECT HANDLING ----------\\n  onGrab(obj) {\\n    // visual cue handled by particles on retrieve mostly\\n  }\\n\\n  onRetrieve(obj) {\\n    if (!obj) return;\\n    // Mystery bag resolution\\n    if (obj.type === \\"mysteryBag\\") {\\n      this.resolveMysteryBag(obj);\\n      obj.removed = true;\\n      return;\\n    }\\n    // TNT object - explode\\n    if (obj.type === \\"tnt\\") {\\n      this.triggerExplosion(obj.x, obj.y, true);\\n      obj.removed = true;\\n      return;\\n    }\\n    // Worm - no reward, breaks combo\\n    if (obj.type === \\"worm\\") {\\n      this.resetCombo();\\n      this.addFloatingText(obj.x, obj.y, \\"Worm! +0\\", \\"#c0706b\\");\\n      obj.removed = true;\\n      AudioManager.sfx.rock();\\n      return;\\n    }\\n    // Rock - Lucky Leaf transformation check\\n    if (obj.type === \\"rock\\") {\\n      if (this.inventory.isLuckyLeafActive()) {\\n        this.inventory.consumeLuckyLeafOnTransform();\\n        this.addFloatingText(obj.x, obj.y, \\"💎 Lucky Leaf! +750\\", \\"#5ee0ff\\");\\n        AudioManager.sfx.leaf();\\n        this.addScoreRaw(750);\\n        this.shake(0.2);\\n        this.flash(0.4, [94, 224, 255]);\\n      } else {\\n        this.addFloatingText(obj.x, obj.y, \\"+50\\", \\"#888\\");\\n        this.addScoreRaw(50);\\n        AudioManager.sfx.rock();\\n      }\\n      this.resetCombo();\\n      obj.removed = true;\\n      return;\\n    }\\n    // Gold / Diamond / Treasure - valuable, builds combo\\n    const value = this.computeObjectValue(obj);\\n    this.addScoreWithCombo(obj, value);\\n    obj.removed = true;\\n  }\\n\\n  computeObjectValue(obj) {\\n    // Most objects have a fixed value\\n    return obj.value;\\n  }\\n\\n  addScoreWithCombo(obj, baseValue) {\\n    // valuable objects: gold, diamond, movingDiamond, goldenTreasure\\n    const isValuable = [\\"smallGold\\", \\"mediumGold\\", \\"largeGold\\", \\"diamond\\", \\"movingDiamond\\", \\"goldenTreasure\\"].includes(obj.type);\\n    if (isValuable) {\\n      this.combo++;\\n      this.updateComboMult();\\n      const earned = Math.round(baseValue * this.comboMult);\\n      this.score += earned;\\n      this.addFloatingText(obj.x, obj.y, `+${earned}`, \\"#ffce3a\\");\\n      if (this.comboMult > 1) {\\n        this.addFloatingText(obj.x, obj.y - 18, `Combo x${this.comboMult}`, \\"#5ec27a\\");\\n      }\\n      // Sound + star threshold notification\\n      if (obj.type === \\"diamond\\" || obj.type === \\"movingDiamond\\") AudioManager.sfx.diamond();\\n      else if (obj.type === \\"goldenTreasure\\") { AudioManager.sfx.diamond(); this.shake(0.3); this.flash(0.5, [255, 215, 0]); }\\n      else AudioManager.sfx.gold();\\n      // Check for star threshold crossed\\n      this.checkStarThreshold();\\n    } else {\\n      this.addScoreRaw(baseValue);\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  addScoreRaw(amount) {\\n    this.score += amount;\\n    if (amount > 0) AudioManager.sfx.gold();\\n    this.checkStarThreshold();\\n  }\\n\\n  updateComboMult() {\\n    if (this.combo >= 8) this.comboMult = 1.3;\\n    else if (this.combo >= 5) this.comboMult = 1.2;\\n    else if (this.combo >= 3) this.comboMult = 1.1;\\n    else this.comboMult = 1.0;\\n    if (this.comboMult > 1) AudioManager.sfx.combo();\\n  }\\n\\n  resetCombo() {\\n    this.combo = 0;\\n    this.comboMult = 1.0;\\n  }\\n\\n  checkStarThreshold() {\\n    const t = Scoring.getThresholds(this.round);\\n    // Mark achieved by simple comparison; the results screen handles final reveal\\n    if (this.score === t.oneStar || this.score === t.twoStar || this.score === t.threeStar) {\\n      AudioManager.sfx.star();\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  // ---------- MYSTERY BAG ----------\\n  resolveMysteryBag(obj) {\\n    AudioManager.sfx.mystery();\\n    const positive = Math.random() < 0.5;\\n    if (positive) {\\n      this.score += 1000;\\n      this.addFloatingText(obj.x, obj.y, \\"Mystery! +1000\\", \\"#5ec27a\\");\\n      AudioManager.sfx.gold();\\n      this.checkStarThreshold();\\n    } else {\\n      // negative - shield can protect\\n      if (this.inventory.hasShield()) {\\n        this.inventory.consumeShield();\\n        this.addFloatingText(obj.x, obj.y, \\"Shield! Blocked -500\\", \\"#5ea2c2\\");\\n        AudioManager.sfx.shield();\\n        this.flash(0.4, [94, 162, 194]);\\n      } else {\\n        this.score = Math.max(0, this.score - 500);\\n        this.addFloatingText(obj.x, obj.y, \\"Mystery! -500\\", \\"#e06464\\");\\n        AudioManager.sfx.fail();\\n        this.shake(0.25);\\n        this.flash(0.4, [224, 100, 100]);\\n      }\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  // ---------- TNT ----------\\n  useTNT() {\\n    if (this.state !== GameState.PLAYING) return;\\n    if (!this.inventory.hasTNT()) { this.toast(\\"No TNT available!\\"); AudioManager.sfx.error(); return; }\\n    if (this.claw.state !== ClawState.SWINGING) { this.toast(\\"Can\'t use TNT while claw is busy!\\"); AudioManager.sfx.error(); return; }\\n\\n    // Find a target Rock. Prefer the one closest to the claw\'s current aim line.\\n    const head = this.claw.headPosition;\\n    // Actually use the anchor + angle projection to find which rock the claw is \\"aiming\\" at.\\n    // We\'ll pick the nearest rock to a point along the current angle at a depth.\\n    let best = null, bestDist = Infinity;\\n    for (const obj of this.objects) {\\n      if (obj.removed || obj.collected) continue;\\n      if (obj.type !== \\"rock\\") continue;\\n      const dx = obj.x - this.claw.anchorX;\\n      const dy = obj.y - this.claw.anchorY;\\n      const dist = Math.hypot(dx, dy);\\n      // weight by closeness to the claw\'s current pointing direction\\n      const ang = Math.atan2(dx, dy);\\n      const angDiff = Math.abs(ang - this.claw.angle);\\n      const score = dist + angDiff * 200;\\n      if (score < bestDist) { bestDist = score; best = obj; }\\n    }\\n    if (!best) { this.toast(\\"No Rock to target with TNT!\\"); AudioManager.sfx.error(); return; }\\n\\n    this.inventory.consumeTNT();\\n    this.triggerExplosion(best.x, best.y, false);\\n    AudioManager.sfx.tnt();\\n    this.shake(0.3);\\n    this.flash(0.5, [255, 120, 0]);\\n    this.toast(\\"TNT! Rock destroyed.\\");\\n    this.updateHUD();\\n  }\\n\\n  triggerExplosion(x, y, fromObject) {\\n    const radius = 90;\\n    // Destroy rocks and worms within radius. Do NOT touch valuable items.\\n    for (const obj of this.objects) {\\n      if (obj.removed || obj.collected) continue;\\n      if (obj.type === \\"rock\\" || obj.type === \\"worm\\") {\\n        const d = Math.hypot(obj.x - x, obj.y - y);\\n        if (d <= radius + obj.size) {\\n          obj.removed = true;\\n          // small puff particle\\n          this.spawnParticles(obj.x, obj.y, 12, \\"#aaa\\");\\n        }\\n      }\\n    }\\n    // explosion particles\\n    this.spawnParticles(x, y, 30, \\"#ff8a3a\\");\\n    this.spawnParticles(x, y, 20, \\"#ffce3a\\");\\n  }\\n\\n  // ---------- LUCKY LEAF ----------\\n  activateLuckyLeaf() {\\n    if (this.state !== GameState.PLAYING) return;\\n    if (this.inventory.luckyLeafActive) { this.toast(\\"Lucky Leaf already active!\\"); return; }\\n    if (!this.inventory.hasLuckyLeaf()) {\\n      // try to use a stored one\\n      if (this.inventory.luckyLeaf > 0) {\\n        this.inventory.activateLuckyLeaf();\\n        AudioManager.sfx.leaf();\\n        this.toast(\\"Lucky Leaf active!   const prevBest = this.save.bestScores[this.round] || 0;\\n    const prevStars = this.save.bestStars[this.round] || 0;\\n    if (this.score > prevBest) this.save.bestScores[this.round] = this.score;\\n    if (stars > prevStars) this.save.bestStars[this.round] = stars;\\n    // Recompute total stars\\n    this.save.totalStars = Object.values(this.save.bestStars).reduce((a, b) => a + b, this.totalGold += this.score;\\n      this.save.totalGold = this.totalGold;\\n    }\\n    this.save.highestRoundReached = Math.max(this.save.highestRoundReached, this.round);\\n    this.save.inventory = this.inventory.serialize();\\n    this.save.clawSpeedLevel = this.clawLevel;\\n    SaveManager.save(this.save);\\n    this.updateMenuSaveInfo();\\n\\n    // Show results screen\\n    this.state = document.getElementById(\\"results-best\\").textContent = `${prevBest} (⭐${prevStars})`;\\n\\n    // Animate star progress meter\\n    this.renderResultsStarProgress();\\n    this.renderResultsStars(stars, passed);\\n    if (passed) AudioManager.sfx.roundComplete();\\n    else AudioManager.sfx.fail();\\n  }\\n\\n  renderResultsStarProgress() {\\n    const wrap = document.getElementById(\\"results-screen\\").classList.add(\\"hidden\\");\\n    // Replaying a passed round: refund the score we added? Spec says don\'t erase gold from previous completed rounds.\\n    // We subtract this round\'s score from totalGold only if it was previously added (it was, in endRound).\\n    // Actually, to keep it simple and fair: replaying re-attempts the round; total gold already includes the previous attempt.\\n    // We don\'t double-add: we set this.score back to 0 and start the round again. The endRound will add the NEW score.\\n    // But that would double count. So we subtract the previous attempt\'s contribution first.\\n    // Simpler: track per-round contribution. Let\'s store lastAttemptGold.\\n    if (this._lastPassedContribution) {\\n      this.totalGold -= this._lastPassedContribution;\\n      this._lastPassedContribution = 0;\\n    }\\n    this.startRound(this.round);\\n  }\\n\\n  showFinal() {\\n    this.state = GameState.FINAL;\\n    document.getElementById(\\"shop-screen\\").classList.add(\\"hidden\\");\\n Camera shake\\n    let sx = 0, sy = 0;\\n    if (this.shakeTime > 0) {\\n      sx = (Math.random() - 0.5) * 8 * this.shakeTime;\\n      sy = (Math.random() - 0.5) * 8 * this.shakeTime;\\n    }\\n    ctx.save();\\n    ctx.translate(sx, sy);\\n\\n    this.drawBackground(ctx, section.bg);\\n    this.player.draw(ctx);\\n    this.claw.draw(ctx);\\n    for (const obj of this.objects) obj.draw(ctx);\\n = \\"#5a4a3a\\"; bottomColor = \\"#3a2a1a\\"; groundColor = \\"#241810\\";\\n        break;\\n      case \\"temple\\":\\n        topColor = \\"#5a3a1a\\"; bottomColor = \\"#3a2410\\"; groundColor = \\"#241008\\";\\n        break;\\n      case \\"mine\\":\\n      default:\\n        topColor = \\"#3d2e1f\\"; bottomColor = \\"#2c2218\\"; groundColor = \\"#1a1410\\";\\n        break;\\n    }\\n
